# Seven strongest rules for building and teaching

Finalized 2026-10-08 after [the collected-source compilation audit](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). These seven recurring patterns are a synthesis of reviewed public evidence, not Karpathy's endorsement, private mind or a claim that every historical implementation followed them. Each has two distinct original source artifacts with exact quotation support. Separate artifacts corroborate recurring advice; they do not establish independent experimental replication. Automatic-caption wording remains uncorrected and is not audio-verified.

**Evidence** below describes his advice or demonstration. **Application** is our practical implementation and grading standard. Repository ownership, model-generated prompts, third-party contributions and syndicated copies were not used as sole proof of personal authorship. Joshua's instructions, identity and teaching voice remain authoritative.

## 1. Explain and reconstruct the mechanism

**Evidence:** His study guidance distinguishes active reconstruction from passive understanding; his backprop article recommends implementation to solidify mechanism-level understanding.

> Reading and understanding IS NOT the same as replicating

[Study advice](../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md), study section; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/other-first-party/cs.stanford.edu_people_karpathy_advice.html.md). Publication date unknown.

> get you to write backprop manually and help you solidify your understanding.

[Yes you should understand backprop](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), conclusion, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md).

**Application:** Explain input, transformation and output using a small example, then invite the learner to reproduce or explain one step. Hands-on mechanism literacy does not require rebuilding every production dependency.

## 2. Start with one correct small case and a baseline

**Evidence:** His recipe recommends getting a specific implementation working before generalizing. In Makemore he starts the neural pipeline with one word rather than the full dataset.

> get that to work, and then generalize it later making sure that I get the same result.

[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “generalize a special case,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).

> [01:06:43] all the words just yet because I want
> [01:06:45] everything to be manageable. Uh so,
> [01:06:47] let's just do the first word, which is

[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). The preceding cue says not to take all words yet.

**Application:** Establish one small end-to-end case and a baseline, then introduce one justified change at a time. A teaching toy and a production system have different success requirements. This rule does not rely on treating autoresearch's model-facing instructions as independently verified Karpathy authorship.

# Validate the skills DB model (claude/ + codex/ subtrees, activations, INDEX).

# Skills — master database

This is the **master database** for skills across Joshua's whole setup — every authored, project, and vendor/plugin skill from every root (Claude, Codex, plugins, per-project `.claude/` and `.agents/`), unioned in one place.

## Storage model

- **Inert.** Nothing here auto-loads. A skill only runs when it's *activated* — **copied** (not symlinked, since 2026-07-17) into a project's `.claude/skills/` or `.agents/skills/` (or into the conservative global roots). This directory is search-and-storage, not a load path. A copy doesn't auto-follow edits to the DB source — re-copy after editing a skill here; `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` flags any activation whose `SKILL.md` has drifted.
- **Split by tool origin, always.** Skills are filed under `claude/` or `codex/` depending on which tool's root they came from — **never merged into one shared folder, even when the content is byte-identical.** If a skill exists for both tools, it gets one entry under each: `claude/<name>/` and `codex/<name>/`. This keeps each tool's set independently editable without silently forking a shared file.
- **A+B for vendor/plugin skills.** A vendor or plugin skill gets (A) a live symlink under `claude/<name>/` or `codex/<name>/` pointing at its real location in the plugin, so it always reflects the installed version, **and** (B) a frozen copy under `claude/_snapshots/<name>/` or `codex/_snapshots/<name>/` for point-in-time reference. Snapshots are refreshed manually, not on a schedule — re-run the refresh only when asked.
- **Authored and project skills** are real files, source of truth, filed the same way under `claude/<name>/` or `codex/<name>/`.

## Save-to-both

Creating a new skill (via skill-creator or by hand) always writes to **both**: the current project's `.claude/skills/` (or `.agents/skills/`) **and** this DB, every time, unconditionally. New skills are project-scoped by default — never auto-promoted to global.

## Finding things

- `claude/README.md` and `codex/README.md` index that tool's skills.
- `INDEX.md` (once generated) is the full one-line-per-skill index across both, grouped by source-type.

See [PARITY.md](PARITY.md) for runtime decisions and [the decision log](../decisions/log.md) for the consolidation rationale.
----

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Validate the skills DB model (replaces the old 'expect exactly 11' check).

Activation model (changed 2026-07-17, Joshua's call): a project activates a DB skill
by COPYING it into `.claude/skills/<name>/` (or `.agents/skills/`), not symlinking.
Copies are real files — portable, committable, no dead links on another machine.
The tradeoff: a copy does not auto-follow DB edits. This script's (e) check exists
because of that tradeoff — it flags copies that have drifted from their DB source.
Symlinks are still tolerated (not an error) for any that predate the change or for
the unrelated vendor-plugin mechanism in the DB itself (skills/claude/_snapshots
pattern), which this script does not touch.

Checks:
  (a) every activation in an active root resolves to real content (no broken symlink);
  (b) no skill lives in an active root un-mirrored (real dir with no DB counterpart);
  (c) INDEX.md covers every top-level DB skill;
  (d) no duplicate canonical name within a subtree;
  (e) a copied (non-symlink) activation's SKILL.md matches its DB source byte-for-byte
      — catches silent drift after the DB is edited but the activation isn't re-synced.
  (f) every multi-segment path cited in CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md resolves on disk
      — routing maps that point at moved or deleted files send sessions hunting.
  (g) no UNDECLARED claude/codex asymmetry. The two subtrees are intentionally unequal,
      so the legitimate split is declared in skills/PARITY.md and only new drift fails.
  (h) effective Claude/Codex skill availability has no undeclared one-runtime drift;
  (i) active instruction scopes have both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md;
  (j) Codex agent TOML files are valid, complete, and activated from the catalog.
Exit 0 = PASS, 1 = FAIL.
"""
from project_paths import project_path, VAULT_ROOT
import os, re, sys, filecmp, tomllib, subprocess
from pathlib import Path

BRAIN = str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2])
DB = os.path.join(BRAIN, "skills")
HOME = str(Path.home())
SPECIAL = {"_snapshots", "_project-forks", "_inactive", "README.md", "INDEX.md"}

# Active roots to audit (only those that exist are checked).
PROJECT_ACTIVE_ROOTS = [
    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".claude", "skills"),
    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".agents", "skills"),
    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".gemini", "skills"),
    str(project_path("email-hq", ".claude", "skills")),
]

# Home-level roots also contain skills installed and managed by external plugins.
# They are included when comparing effective Claude/Codex availability, but they
# are not required to be mirrored into this project's catalog.
RUNTIME_ACTIVE_ROOTS = {
    "claude": [os.path.join(HOME, ".claude", "skills"),
               os.path.join(BRAIN, ".claude", "skills")],
    "codex": [os.path.join(HOME, ".agents", "skills"),
              os.path.join(BRAIN, ".agents", "skills")],
}

def db_names(sub):
    base = os.path.join(DB, sub)
    if not os.path.isdir(base):
        return set()
    return {n for n in os.listdir(base)
            if n not in SPECIAL and (os.path.isdir(os.path.join(base, n)) or os.path.islink(os.path.join(base, n)))}

    if a == b:
        return True
    pattern = r"\]\(([^)]+)\)"
    left, right = list(re.finditer(pattern, a)), list(re.finditer(pattern, b))
    if len(left) != len(right):
        return False

        return False

description: Measure description budget and explicit-only triggers
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/38a93a63-e397-478e-9df6-b7085bedd58f/scratchpad" && python -c "
import json,collections,re
d=json.load(open('inventory.json',encoding='utf-8'))
vis={}
for r in d:
    if r['root'] in ('claude:global','claude:project'):
        vis.setdefault(r['dir'],[]).append(r)
print('claude-visible local skills:',len(vis))
both=[n for n,v in vis.items() if len(v)>1]
print('in both global and project:',len(both))
# drift between global and project copies
import hashlib,os
dr=[]
for n in both:
    a=open(os.path.join(vis[n][0]['path'],'SKILL.md'),'rb').read().replace(b'\r\n',b'\n')
    b=open(os.path.join(vis[n][1]['path'],'SKILL.md'),'rb').read().replace(b'\r\n',b'\n')
    if a!=b: dr.append(n)
print('global vs project SKILL.md differ:',len(dr),dr)
recs=[v[-1] for v in vis.values()]
dm=[r for r in recs if r.get('description') is not None]
tot=sum(len(r['description']) for r in dm)
print('total desc chars',tot,'mean',tot//max(1,len(dm)))
pat=re.compile(r'(?i)use only when .{0,40}explicitly|only when (the user|joshua) explicitly|does not (trigger|activate)')
narrow=[r['dir'] for r in dm if pat.search(r['description'])]
print('explicit-only/negative-trigger descriptions:',len(narrow))
print(' '.join(sorted(narrow)))
dmi=[r['dir'] for r in recs if 'disable-model-invocation' in (r.get('keys') or [])]
print('disable-model-invocation:',len(dmi))
"

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Separate wording-only drift from substantive drift
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/38a93a63-e397-478e-9df6-b7085bedd58f/scratchpad" && cat > drift.py <<'EOF'
import json, os, re, sys, difflib
d = json.load(open('inventory.json', encoding='utf-8'))
idx = {}
for r in d:
    idx.setdefault(r['root'], {})[r['dir']] = r

def read(r):
    p = os.path.join(r['path'], 'SKILL.md')
    return open(p, encoding='utf-8', errors='replace').read().replace('\r\n', '\n')

CONTRACT = re.compile(r"\n## (?:Local|Current AI OS) execution contract[^\n]*\n.*?(?=\n## |\n# |\Z)", re.S)
def norm(t):
    t = CONTRACT.sub("\n", t)
    t = re.sub(r"\n## Workflow\n", "\n", t)
    t = re.sub(r"[ \t]+\n", "\n", t)
    t = re.sub(r"\n{2,}", "\n\n", t)
    return t.strip()

pairs = [('claude:global', 'db:claude'), ('codex:global-agents', 'db:codex'), ('codex:global-codex', 'db:codex')]
out = {}
for g, db in pairs:
    G, D = idx[g], idx[db]
    contract_only, substantive = [], []
    for n in sorted(set(G) & set(D)):
        a, b = read(G[n]), read(D[n])
        if a == b:
            continue
        if norm(a) == norm(b):
            contract_only.append(n)
        else:
            sm = difflib.SequenceMatcher(None, norm(a), norm(b))
            substantive.append((n, round(sm.ratio(), 3)))
    out[g] = {'contract_only': contract_only, 'substantive': substantive}
    print(f"== {g} vs {db}: contract-only {len(contract_only)}, substantive {len(substantive)}")
    print('  substantive:', ' '.join(f"{n}({r})" for n, r in substantive))
json.dump(out, open('drift.json', 'w'), indent=1)
EOF
python drift.py
[/external_agent_tool_call]

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Count skill usage across all Claude and Codex session logs
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/38a93a63-e397-478e-9df6-b7085bedd58f/scratchpad" && cat > usage.py <<'EOF'
"""Count skill invocations: Claude Skill tool calls and Codex SKILL.md reads, across all local session logs."""
import json, os, re, sys, collections
from pathlib import Path
HOME = Path.home()
claude = collections.Counter(); claude_last = {}
codex = collections.Counter(); codex_last = {}
skill_call = re.compile(r'"name":"Skill","input":\{"skill":"([^"]+)"')
files = list((HOME / ".claude" / "projects").rglob("*.jsonl"))
for f in files:
    try:
        text = f.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    except OSError:
        continue
    for m in skill_call.finditer(text):
        claude[m.group(1)] += 1
        claude_last[m.group(1)] = max(claude_last.get(m.group(1), ""), f.stat().st_mtime.__str__())
read_skill = re.compile(r'skills[\\/]+([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)[\\/]+SKILL\.md')
cfiles = list((HOME / ".codex" / "sessions").rglob("*.jsonl")) if (HOME / ".codex" / "sessions").is_dir() else []
for f in cfiles:
    try:
        text = f.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    except OSError:
        continue
    seen = set(m.group(1) for m in read_skill.finditer(text))
    for s in seen:
        codex[s] += 1  # sessions that read this SKILL.md at least once
json.dump({"claude_files": len(files), "codex_files": len(cfiles),
           "claude": claude.most_common(), "codex_sessions": codex.most_common()},
          open("usage.json", "w"), indent=1)
print("claude transcripts", len(files), "distinct skills invoked", len(claude))
print("codex sessions", len(cfiles), "distinct skills read", len(codex))
print("CLAUDE top 40:", claude.most_common(40))
print("CODEX top 60:", codex.most_common(60))
EOF
python usage.py
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Find never-used skills excluding bulk audit sessions
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/38a93a63-e397-478e-9df6-b7085bedd58f/scratchpad" && cat > usage2.py <<'EOF'
import json, re, collections
from pathlib import Path
HOME = Path.home()
read_skill = re.compile(r'skills[\\/]+([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)[\\/]+SKILL\.md')
codex = collections.Counter(); bulk = 0
for f in (HOME / ".codex" / "sessions").rglob("*.jsonl"):
    seen = set(m.group(1) for m in read_skill.finditer(f.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")))
    if len(seen) > 10:
        bulk += 1
        continue
    for s in seen:
        codex[s] += 1
cfg = json.load(open(HOME / ".claude.json", encoding="utf-8"))
claude_usage = {k.split(":")[-1]: v["usageCount"] for k, v in cfg["skillUsage"].items()}
u = json.load(open("usage.json"))
claude_calls = {k.split(":")[-1]: v for k, v in u["claude"]}
used = set(claude_usage) | set(claude_calls) | set(codex)
inv = json.load(open("inventory.json", encoding="utf-8"))
vis = {}
for r in inv:
    if r["root"] in ("claude:global", "claude:project") and r.get("description") is not None:
        vis[r["dir"]] = r
dmi = {n for n, r in vis.items() if "disable-model-invocation" in (r.get("keys") or [])}
model_visible = set(vis) - dmi
never = sorted(model_visible - used)
print("bulk-audit codex sessions excluded:", bulk)
print("codex non-bulk distinct skills:", len(codex))
print("model-visible local Claude skills:", len(model_visible), "| ever used anywhere:", len(model_visible & used), "| never used:", len(never))
groups = collections.defaultdict(list)
for n in never:
    g = n.split("-")[0] if n.split("-")[0] in ("recipe", "persona", "gws", "firecrawl", "hyperframes", "lsat", "writing", "grill", "to", "setup") else "other"
    groups[g].append(n)
for g, ns in sorted(groups.items(), key=lambda kv: -len(kv[1])):
    print(f"\n[{g}] {len(ns)}: " + " ".join(ns))
json.dump({"codex_nonbulk": codex.most_common(), "never": never, "used": sorted(used)}, open("usage2.json", "w"), indent=1)
EOF

import json, re, collections
from pathlib import Path
HOME = Path.home()
pat = re.compile(r'"file_path":"[^"]*?skills[\\/]+([a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)[\\/]+SKILL\.md"')
reads = collections.Counter(); bulk = 0
for f in (HOME / ".claude" / "projects").rglob("*.jsonl"):
    seen = set(m.group(1) for m in pat.finditer(f.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")))
    if len(seen) > 10:
        bulk += 1; continue
    for s in seen: reads[s] += 1
u2 = json.load(open("usage2.json"))
never = set(u2["never"])
newly = sorted(never & set(reads))
print("claude bulk sessions excluded:", bulk, "| skills read via Read tool:", len(reads))
print("previously 'never used' but read via Read tool:", newly)
never2 = sorted(never - set(reads))
print("never used after this correction:", len(never2))
json.dump({"never": never2, "claude_reads": reads.most_common()}, open("usage3.json", "w"), indent=1)
EOF

# Skill parity baseline

## 2026-10-09: Persona Agent Creator

`persona-agent-creator` is catalogued and activated as matching project copies in both runtimes. Its host-aware factory procedure requires actual native person-agent registration and an independently grading entry skill. It reuses existing ingest/schema conventions, derives each person's own rules, and explicitly tests new hook scope instead of inheriting Karpathy's hardcoded guard. Codex invokes `$persona-agent-creator`; generated person agents use each host's native configuration. No global activation was added.

Declared single-runtime skills. `check-skills.py` fails on **undeclared** asymmetry only:
when you deliberately add a skill to one runtime and not the other, add it here in the same
commit. When a skill shows up as undeclared drift, either activate it for both runtimes or
declare it here **with a reason**.

Every entry below carries its justification. An entry without one is not a decision, it is a
snapshot — which is what this file was until the audit noted below.

**Audited 2026-09-12.** The Windows parity pass repaired the cross-platform validator,
catalogued `kling-cli` for both runtimes, and ported 25 previously undeclared Claude-only
skills to Codex: `academy-guide`, `algorithmic-art`, `brand-guidelines`, `canvas-design`,
`claude-api`, `claude-handoff`, `code-review`, `discernment-nudge`, `doc-coauthoring`,
`docx`, `implement-spec`, `internal-comms`, `loop-me`, `mcp-builder`,
`migrate-to-shoehorn`, `pdf`, `pptx`, `retro`, `session-recap`,
`setup-ts-deep-modules`, `slack-gif-creator`, `theme-factory`,
`web-artifacts-builder`, `wizard`, and `xlsx`. Current catalog counts:
**claude=211 codex=188**.

Note that DB parity is not activation. The DB is the complete catalog; Codex's activated set
in `.agents/skills/` is a deliberately small ops core. Porting a skill here does not activate
it, and is not meant to.

1	---
2	name: booking-link
3	description: Use whenever Joshua wants a one-off booking link for LSAT U — "make me a link for Dakota at 7:15 tomorrow", "send her a link to book tonight", "one-off link, $60", "short notice link", "I need a link she can schedule herself with". Mints a single-use URL that sells exactly one session at a price you set, optionally pinned to one exact time, and closes after she books. Also covers listing and revoking existing links.
4	---
5	
6	## Current AI OS execution contract
7	
8	Resolve this repository root from AGENTS.md and the application from its registered sibling project. Read credentials only through the central private .env loader. Legacy home-relative application or secret paths below are examples, not current authorities. Draft/send authority is unchanged.
9	
10	# One-off booking links
11	
12	A URL Joshua sends to one person. She opens it, books one session, pays, and
13	gets the Google Meet invite from the ordinary booking flow. Then the link
14	closes.
15	
16	## Authorization
17	
18	Minting a link is pre-authorized. Joshua asking for a link means make it now —
19	do not ask permission, ask only for the facts you actually need.
20	
21	**Never send the link anywhere.** Print it and hand it to Joshua. He decides who
22	gets it. A link is a Bearer [REDACTED]: whoever holds it can book that slot.
23	
24	## What you need before running
25	
26	Only two things are required, and one has a default:
27	
28	- **Price** in dollars. Required — links always charge, and the standard rate is
29	  $45 but a given person may pay something else. Ask if it was not said.
30	- **Who it is for.** Used only for the admin-side note, never in the URL. If
31	  Joshua did not say, use a short description.
32	
33	Everything else is optional. Ask for nothing you were not given.

[external_agent_tool_result]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Pull booking-time student submissions (files + notes) from the LSAT U backend
# into students/<name>/submissions/. Idempotent: a per-student manifest records
# what has already been pulled, so re-running only fetches new submissions.
#
# Auth: logs in with the admin creds in ~/ai-os/.env against GoTrue (password
# grant), then calls the booking-pass-admin edge function exactly as the admin
# UI does. Files come back as short-lived signed URLs from the private bucket.
#
# Usage: pull-submissions.sh            (pull everything new)
#        pull-submissions.sh --dry-run  (list what would be pulled, write nothing)
set -euo pipefail

AIOS="${AIOS_ROOT:-$HOME/ai-os}"
DRY_RUN=0
[[ "${1:-}" == "--dry-run" ]] && DRY_RUN=1

# --- env: admin creds (root .env) + supabase url/anon (lsatu .env) ------------
set -a
# shellcheck disable=SC1090
source "$AIOS/.env"
# shellcheck disable=SC1091
LSATU_ROOT="${LSATU_ROOT:-$(dirname "$AIOS")/ai-os-projects/lsatu}"
source "$LSATU_ROOT/.env"
set +a

SUPABASE_URL="${VITE_SUPABASE_URL:?missing VITE_SUPABASE_URL}"
ANON="${VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY:?missing VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY}"
EMAIL="${LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL:?missing LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL}"
PASS="${LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD:?missing LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD}"

need() { command -v "$1" >/dev/null || { echo "need $1 installed" >&2; exit 1; }; }
need jq; need curl

# --- 1) login -> access token -------------------------------------------------
TOKEN=$(curl -s "$SUPABASE_URL/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password" \
  -H "apikey: [REDACTED] -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d "{\"email\":\"$EMAIL\",\"password\":\"$PASS\"}" | jq -r '.access_token // empty')
[[ -n "$TOKEN" ]] || { echo "login failed" >&2; exit 1; }
[/external_agent_tool_result]

1	---
2	name: pull-submissions
3	description: Pull booking-time student submissions (files + comments) from the LSAT U backend into students/<name>/submissions/. Use when Joshua says "pull submissions", "get the booking uploads", "sync student submissions", or as part of /pickup. On-demand only — no cron, no watcher.
4	---
5	
6	## Current AI OS execution contract
7	
8	Resolve current booking mirror paths and relationship IDs from the canonical root/live systems. Use stable source IDs for idempotent filing and do not infer a student identity from a loose name match.
9	
10	# Pull Submissions
11	
12	When a student books a session through their `/book/<slug>` link, the confirm step lets them optionally attach work (any file, up to 5) and leave a comment. Those land in the LSAT U backend (Supabase storage + `external_work_uploads`, comment on `booking_pass_redemptions.student_note`) and are surfaced in the Bookings admin tab and the booking confirmation email to Joshua.
13	
14	This skill is the **on-demand pull** that mirrors those submissions into the ai-os brain, so a student's work lives alongside their transcripts.
15	
16	## What it does
17	
18	1. Logs in with the admin creds in `~/ai-os/.env` (`LSATPREP_ADMIN_*`) against GoTrue, exactly as the admin UI authenticates.
19	2. Calls `booking-pass-admin` → `list_bookings`, keeps every booking that has a note or attached files.
20	3. For each new one, downloads the files (via short-lived signed URLs from `get_booking_uploads`) and writes the comment, into `students/<name>/submissions/`.
21	4. Records each pulled redemption in a per-student `.pulled.json` manifest so re-running only fetches what's new (idempotent).
22	
23	## Run it
24	
25	```bash
26	bash ~/ai-os/skills/claude/pull-submissions/pull-submissions.sh            # pull everything new
27	bash ~/ai-os/skills/claude/pull-submissions/pull-submissions.sh --dry-run  # list only, write nothing
28	```
29	
30	Requires `curl` and `jq`. Reads `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` / `VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` from `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/.env` and the admin creds from the root `.env` (both sourced with absolute paths — cwd is unreliable).
31	
32	## Output layout
33	
34	```
35	students/<name>/submissions/
36	  .pulled.json                       # manifest of redemption ids already pulled
37	  2026-07-19_a1b2c3d4_note.md        # the student's comment (if any)
38	  2026-07-19_a1b2c3d4_PT155.pdf      # each attached file, prefixed date_redemption
39	```
40	
41	Student name → folder slug is derived from the pass name (lowercased, hyphenated). If the folder doesn't exist yet it's created; reconcile a new folder against `students/README.md` naming if the student already has a record under a different slug.
42	
43	## Notes
44	
45	- On-demand by design. Do not add a cron or launchd watcher — Joshua decided the pull is manual (can run inside `/pickup`).
46	- The backend is the source of truth; this is a mirror. Never delete backend rows from here.
47	- Files download through 10-minute signed URLs; if a download 403s, just re-run (a fresh URL is minted each call).
48	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

1	---
2	name: lsat-u-platform-actions
3	description: Operate LSAT U from AI OS. Assign complete interactive Classroom lessons directly through the local lsat-u-lessons MCP server, verify the saved content and student visibility headlessly, and log the assignment. Use the Node CLI for other supported actions.
4	---
5	
6	## Current AI OS execution contract
7	
8	Resolve the actual host browser/CLI before use, preserve marker-based idempotency, exact recipient/action verification and live readback. Do not replace a working transport simply because a reference names another runtime.
9	
10	For drills, messages, student data, access, signups, due dates, unassign, and unsend, use `assign-lsatu-work`. On Windows, invoke its Node entry point directly instead of relying on Bash being on PATH.
11	
12	
13	# LSAT U Platform Actions
14	
15	Joshua's workplace is this brain workspace. LSAT U operations should run headlessly when a supported tool exists. The browser is for checking what a person sees.
16	
17	## Mechanism
18	
19	For an interactive HTML lesson, call the `lsat-u-lessons` MCP tool `publish_interactive_lesson` with the student's exact email, absolute HTML path, and title. The local stdio server is `system/scripts/lsatu-lesson-mcp.mjs`; Codex registration is `lsat-u-lessons`. It calls `system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs post-lesson`, which authenticates with the existing admin credentials, posts through `mcp_post_classroom_note`, verifies the saved HTML exactly and student visibility, and then archives only earlier versions with the same lesson marker. A repeat of the current version returns the same item without another notification. Use `dry_run: true` when the student or replacement is uncertain; otherwise publish in one call. MCP tools newly registered during a session may only appear in a new Codex session; the direct Node command is the immediate fallback.
20	
21	The HTML must be a complete document inside AI OS, under 500 KB, with `<meta name="lsatu-interactive-lesson" content="unique-lesson-id">`. Reuse the same lesson ID for revisions of that lesson; use a different ID for a different lesson. Inline the CSS, JavaScript, fonts, and visual assets. Preflight rejects nonembedded resources; do not bypass it or paste HTML into an ordinary sanitized note. The already-live Classroom renderer loads marked HTML in a sandboxed iframe with scripts and forms enabled. **Daily lesson posting is a content operation and never requires Lovable.** Only an actual app renderer change follows the separate app deployment rules.
22	
23	Routine verification is headless: the publisher reads the new item back, compares its HTML byte for byte, checks that it is visible to the intended student, and confirms one active version. The result includes its route and SHA-256. Use Codex's in-app browser only when a visual or interaction defect is suspected or the page design or renderer changed. A teacher login cannot open another student's `/classroom/item/<id>` route directly. Do not claim a student-session visual check unless one was actually performed.
24	
25	The student opens the full lesson from Classroom **Notes and recaps**, the in-app notification, or its Inbox link. Classroom Home may show a recent note as a “Read” row, but it has a four-row limit shared with urgent work, so do not promise that every assigned note stays on Home. This does not affect the note's visibility in Notes and recaps or its direct item route.
26	
27	## UI fallback routing
28	
29	The current student verification route is `/teacher/students/<student-id>/notes`. Its “Posted to their Classroom” section shows active notes and labels archived notes as hidden from the student. Use the browser's normal controls for any unsupported action, then verify the saved state through a fresh read. In Codex, use the in-app browser, not Chrome. Do not rely on old `/teacher?ws=` routes or old browser-tool names.
30	
31	## Order of operations (every action)
32	
33	1. **Identify the student** exactly ...

**React controlled-input rule (hard-won 2026-07-16):** `form_input` sets DOM values
but often does NOT update React state, leaving submit buttons silently disabled and
producing zero network traffic. For every form:

1. Click the field and type with real keystrokes (`computer` type action); for
   `<select>`s, click to focus then choose with keyboard (first-letter + Enter) or a
   real option click.
2. Before clicking submit, confirm the button is actually enabled (full-opacity, or
   check `disabled` via read_page).
3. After clicking, confirm via `read_network_requests` that the mutation actually
   fired (POST to `…supabase.co/functions/v1/…` or `/rest/v1/…`) and returned 2xx.

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Validate the skills DB model (replaces the old 'expect exactly 11' check).
3	
4	Activation model (changed 2026-07-17, Joshua's call): a project activates a DB skill
5	by COPYING it into `.claude/skills/<name>/` (or `.agents/skills/`), not symlinking.
6	Copies are real files — portable, committable, no dead links on another machine.
7	The tradeoff: a copy does not auto-follow DB edits. This script's (e) check exists
8	because of that tradeoff — it flags copies that have drifted from their DB source.
9	Symlinks are still tolerated (not an error) for any that predate the change or for
10	the unrelated vendor-plugin mechanism in the DB itself (skills/claude/_snapshots
11	pattern), which this script does not touch.
12	
13	Checks:
14	  (a) every activation in an active root resolves to real content (no broken symlink);
15	  (b) no skill lives in an active root un-mirrored (real dir with no DB counterpart);
16	  (c) INDEX.md covers every top-level DB skill;
17	  (d) no duplicate canonical name within a subtree;
18	  (e) a copied (non-symlink) activation's SKILL.md matches its DB source byte-for-byte
19	      — catches silent drift after the DB is edited but the activation isn't re-synced.
20	  (f) every multi-segment path cited in CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md resolves on disk
21	      — routing maps that point at moved or deleted files send sessions hunting.
22	  (g) no UNDECLARED claude/codex asymmetry. The two subtrees are intentionally unequal,
23	      so the legitimate split is declared in skills/PARITY.md and only new drift fails.
24	  (h) effective Claude/Codex skill availability has no undeclared one-runtime drift;
25	  (i) active instruction scopes have both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md;
26	  (j) Codex agent TOML files are valid, complete, and activated from the catalog.
27	Exit 0 = PASS, 1 = FAIL.
28	"""
29	from project_paths import project_path, VAULT_ROOT
30	import os, re, sys, filecmp, tomllib, subprocess
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

Your skills weren't unpopular because they're bad. Most of them couldn't be seen. I found two causes, fixed both, and confirmed the fixes in real Claude Code sessions. Full report: [SUMMARY.md](audits/skills-2026-10-12-karpathy/SUMMARY.md).

## Why unused skills stayed unused

Claude Code only shows the model a skill's description while a budget lasts: 1% of the context window, which is 30,000 characters here. It hands descriptions out by recent use first, then alphabetically. A live debug run measured 403 skills wanting 126,322 characters.

So a skill you've never used gets only its name, can't trigger on its own, and stays unused. `academy-guide` and `brand-guidelines` had full descriptions because they start with A and B. `transcript-intake`, `post-session-recap`, `pickup`, `level-up`, `guided-drill-assignment` and `lsat-u-platform-actions` showed up as bare names. Across 669 past sessions, only 88 skills were ever used.

**Fix:** new settings in the vault's `.claude/settings.json`. The budget goes to 1.5%. 69 vendor skills you've never used (Workspace recipes and personas, enterprise admin tools) are hidden from the model, but still run if you type `/name`. 183 never-used generic skills show by name only.

**Verified in a fresh session:** 10 of 10 sampled core skills now show their descriptions, up from 0. Cost: about 3,750 more tokens per message, in AI OS sessions only.

## Many skills Claude loaded were stale

When a skill exists in both your home folder and the vault, Claude loads the home copy. 142 home copies had drifted from the catalog while the validator still said PASS. Some were actively wrong:

- **superwhisper** said you record with Ctrl+Space. You use Left Alt.
- **wrapup** wrote log entries at the top of the log, in an old format.
- **bootcamp-builder** said to "commit and push without asking" after a green build.
- **lsat-u-platform-actions** still drove Chrome by hand and said there was no backend path.
- **lsat-lab-mega-set** deleted wrong practice sets automatically.

I synced 16 skills from the catalog, after backing up every file and checking that nothing unique would be lost. That check caught a lesson the catalog had dropped, the React form-input rule, so I restored it.

## Other repairs

- **pull-submissions** was broken on Windows. I reproduced the failure first, without touching your backend, then fixed the path. It still needs `jq`, which isn't installed.
- **kling-cli** was missing for Claude. Restored.
- **assign-lsatu-work** had wrong credential notes. Its description is cut from 1,086 to 851 characters, with the trigger phrases kept.
- **Gemini's `audit`** was missing its rubric. Added.
- **The validator** runs on Windows now. It fails when a home copy hides a newer vault copy (tested with a planted stale copy). It no longer skips 26 skills just because `PARITY.md` mentions them.

## Wiki scan

The vault's lint checked 3,896 pages and found 2 dead links, both in old log entries. Four web clips in `raw/` were never ingested. `wiki/tasks.md` hasn't been updated since 2026-10-08.

## What I left for you

1. 121 home copies still differ from the catalog, mostly vendor and generic skills. The validator now shows this count on every run.
2. Install `jq` if you want pull-submissions to work (`winget install jqlang.jq`).
3. Twenty skills on your claude.ai account duplicate local ones, such as `anthropic-skills:pdf`. Removing them is an account change.
4. `level-up` exists in two different designs. Pick one.
5. Ingest the four clips and update `tasks.md` at the next wrapup.

Midway through, another session installed 36 Expo and SEO skills that you'd authorized. I didn't touch them. They would have pushed three of your core skills back to bare names. Expo's own `expo-overview` skill is designed to route to its sub-skills, so those sub-skills now show by name only.

## Assumptions

- "Never used" means on this laptop. MacBook sessions aren't in these logs, and any name-only setting is easy to undo.
- I verified Claude's loading behavior. Codex's wasn't tested; I made every copy identical so it shouldn't matter.
- I didn't use the karpathy subagent. This was a repair of your skill system, not a teaching question, so I applied the method myself and graded the work against the seven rules in `wiki/karpathy/rules.md`.

## Seven-rule grades

1. **Mechanism:** pass. The budget rules and load order come from the installed Claude Code program, confirmed in live runs.
2. **Small baseline:** pass. I measured before changing anything and reproduced single failures first.
3. **Predict and compare:** pass. Live runs matched every prediction, except one I logged: I expected YAML errors and found none.
4. **Counterexample:** pass. Two of my own bugs were caught and fixed, including a rule that would have hidden your `persona-agent-creator`.
5. **Verified behavior:** pass for Claude. The proof is what the running app loaded. The Codex runtime wasn't tested.
6. **Assumptions:** pass. Listed above.
7. **Simplicity:** pass. I used Claude Code's own settings plus two small scripts.

## What I ran

A scan of 1,910 skill copies and 669 session logs, three headless Claude runs with debug logging, a before-and-after test of pull-submissions, the validator plus a planted failure, and the wiki lint.

## What came out

The validator passes (311 Claude, 299 Codex, 222 Gemini skills) with drift warnings. A rerun of the sync makes zero changes. All sampled core skills are described.

## What I changed

The vault's listing settings, the validator, six catalog skills, 16 home-folder skills, `kling-cli`, Gemini's `audit`, the skills README, a log entry and two memory notes. Nothing deleted, sent or committed.

[external_agent_tool_result]
  -d "{\"email\":\"$EMAIL\",\"password\":\"$PASS\"}" | jq -r '.access_token // empty')
[[ -n "$TOKEN" ]] || { echo "login failed" >&2; exit 1; }

admin() { # action json -> response
  curl -s "$SUPABASE_URL/functions/v1/booking-pass-admin" \
    -H "Authorization: [REDACTED] $TOKEN" -H "apikey: [REDACTED] -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    -d "$1"
}

slugify() { echo "$1" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]' | sed -E 's/[^a-z0-9]+/-/g; s/^-+|-+$//g'; }

# --- 2) list bookings, keep those with a note or files ------------------------
BOOKINGS=$(admin '{"action":"list_bookings"}' | jq -c '[.bookings[] | select((.upload_count // 0) > 0 or (.student_note // "" | length > 0))]')
COUNT=$(echo "$BOOKINGS" | jq 'length')
echo "Found $COUNT booking(s) with a submission."

pulled_new=0
for row in $(echo "$BOOKINGS" | jq -c '.[]'); do
  RID=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.id')
  NOTE=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.student_note // ""')
  NUP=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.upload_count // 0')
  START=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.start_at')
  NAME=$(echo "$row" | jq -r '.booking_passes.student_name // .student_name_snapshot // .booking_passes.student_email // .student_email_snapshot // "unknown-student"')
  DATE=$(echo "$START" | cut -c1-10)
  SLUG=$(slugify "$NAME")

  DEST="$AIOS/students/$SLUG/submissions"
  MANIFEST="$DEST/.pulled.json"
  # already pulled this redemption?
  if [[ -f "$MANIFEST" ]] && jq -e --arg r "$RID" 'index($r)' "$MANIFEST" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    continue
  fi

  echo "  • $NAME ($DATE): note=$([[ -n "$NOTE" ]] && echo yes || echo no) files=$NUP"
  [[ $DRY_RUN -eq 1 ]] && continue

  mkdir -p "$DEST"
  BASE="$DEST/${DATE}_${RID:0:8}"

  # note file
  if [[ -n "$NOTE" ]]; then
    { echo "# Booking submission note — $NAME"; echo; echo "- Session: $START"; echo "- Redemption: $RID"; echo; echo "$NOTE"; } > "${BASE}_note.md"
  fi

  # files via signed urls
  if [[ "$NUP" -gt 0 ]]; then
    UPLOADS=$(admin "{\"action\":\"get_booking_uploads\",\"redemption_id\":\"$RID\"}")
    for u in $(echo "$UPLOADS" | jq -c '.uploads[]'); do
      FNAME=$(echo "$u" | jq -r '.file_name')
      URL=$(echo "$u" | jq -r '.url // empty')
      [[ -n "$URL" ]] || { echo "    (no url for $FNAME)"; continue; }
      curl -s -L -o "${BASE}_${FNAME}" "$URL" && echo "    ↓ ${DATE}_${RID:0:8}_${FNAME}"
    done
  fi

  # record in manifest
  if [[ -f "$MANIFEST" ]]; then
    jq --arg r "$RID" '. + [$r] | unique' "$MANIFEST" > "$MANIFEST.tmp" && mv "$MANIFEST.tmp" "$MANIFEST"
  else
    echo "[\"$RID\"]" > "$MANIFEST"
  fi
  pulled_new=$((pulled_new+1))
done

echo "Done. Pulled $pulled_new new submission(s)."
[/external_agent_tool_result]

\"\"\"Split review-class drift: provider-swap corruption only, updater-owned packs, and the rest.\"\"\"
import json, re
from pathlib import Path
HERE = Path(__file__).parent
V = HERE.parents[1]
HOME = Path.home()
ROOT = {'home-claude': (HOME/'.claude/skills','claude'), 'home-agents': (HOME/'.agents/skills','codex'), 'home-codex': (HOME/'.codex/skills','codex')}
CONTRACT = re.compile(r'^## (Local|Current AI OS) execution contract')
STALE = re.compile(r'~/ai-os\b|~/\.Codex|/Users/joshuaf|/mnt/d/|projects/lsatu/|archive/wiki-pre-rebuild')
UPDATER = set('ask-matt decision-mapping edit-article handoff claude-handoff implement implement-spec improve-codebase-architecture loop-me qa request-refactor-plan resolving-merge-conflicts retro review scaffold-exercises setup-pre-commit setup-ts-deep-modules tdd teach to-prd to-questionnaire wayfinder writing-beats writing-great-skills codebase-design design-an-interface prototype brainstorming executing-plans finishing-a-development-branch requesting-code-review systematic-debugging test-driven-development using-git-worktrees using-superpowers writing-plans writing-skills'.split())
def lines(t, swap=False):
    out, skip = [], False
    for ln in t.replace('\r\n','\n').split('\n'):
        if CONTRACT.match(ln): skip=True; continue
        if skip and ln.startswith(('## ','# ')): skip=False
        s = re.sub(r'\s+',' ',ln.strip())
        if swap: s = s.replace('Codex Code','Claude Code').replace('Codex','Claude').replace('.Codex','.claude')
        if skip or not s or s=='## Workflow' or s.startswith('description:'): continue
        out.append(s)
    return out
d = json.load(open(HERE/'drift-classes.json', encoding='utf-8'))
res = {'corruption-only': [], 'updater-owned': [], 'decide': []}
for r in d['review']:
    root, rt = ROOT[r['install']]
    a = (root/r['skill']/'SKILL.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8', errors='replace')
    b = (V/'skills'/rt/r['skill']/'SKILL.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8', errors='replace')
    cat = set(lines(b, True))
    left = [l for l in lines(a, True) if l not in cat and not STALE.search(l)]
    key = 'updater-owned' if r['skill'] in UPDATER else ('corruption-only' if not left else 'decide')
    res[key].append({**r, 'left': len(left), 'left_sample': left[:3]})
json.dump(res, open(HERE/'review-classes.json','w',encoding='utf-8'), indent=1)
for k,v in res.items(): print(k, len(v), ' '.join(sorted({x['skill'] for x in v})))
EOF

## jq and pull-submissions

- **Installed:** jq 1.8.2 via winget. Open a new terminal before using it; your current shells won't see it on PATH yet.
- **New bug:** with jq in place, the script hit a second bug that would have broken it on the Mac too. It looped with `for row in $(jq ...)`, which splits any student note at its spaces. I rewrote both loops to read one record per line, and removed the Windows line endings jq adds to its output.
- **Verified:** a dry run lists Celia (2026-10-12) and Vinay Kalva (2026-08-10), each with a note and no files, and exits cleanly. Dry run means nothing was written.
- **Backend contact:** a dry run signs in to LSAT U read-only, and two runs reached it. The first was meant to be blocked by a fake `curl`, but Git Bash ignored the fake and used the real one. The second, the verification run above, was deliberate.

## Drifted skills

Before this round there were 299 drifted copies (121 Claude, 125 `~/.agents`, 53 `~/.codex`). I synced 40 skills (104 files across 88 installed copies), all backed up first:

- **30 skills, lossless:** the catalog already had everything in the loaded copy, or the only differences were dead paths.
- **10 skills, by hand:** the catalog was clearly the better version:
  - `design-taste-frontend`, `high-end-visual-design` and `minimalist-ui` forced animation on every card.
  - `prompt-architect-refiner` repeated approval prompts.
  - `lovable-prompt` pointed at a corrupted `.Codex/safe-push.sh` path.
  - `chat-export` promised a full transcript even when the logs were missing.
  - `consultation-followup-drafts` used Mac paths.
  - `obsidian-vault`, `lsat-u-topic-builder` and `canvas-design` were also older.

The rest (89 Claude, 93 `~/.agents`, 29 `~/.codex`) I left on purpose:

- **82 newer vendor copies,** mostly Google Workspace 0.22.5 against the catalog's 0.22.1. Here the catalog is the stale side.
- **76 Matt Pocock and Superpowers copies.** Their scheduled updaters manage them and deliberately skip locally edited copies, so overwriting them would fight the updaters.
- **Copies that are newer or a different design:**
  - `watch` has the 2026-10-11 setup preflight.
  - `impeccable`'s loaded copy may be the newer upstream.
  - The catalog's `site-teardown` breaks the description limit.
  - `skill-creator`, `second-brain`, `graphify` and the Codex `grill-me` are different designs.

**Your call: `level-up`.** It's the one skill where you need to choose between two versions. The generic Nate Herk version is what currently loads. The catalog has the version adapted to your workspace: students instead of clients, and drafts-only for anything a student or prospect reads.

The validator passes, and the log is updated. Classification results are in `audits/skills-2026-10-12-karpathy/`: `drift-classes.json` and `review-classes.json`.

# Ingest sources into the current brain

One public skill owns discovery, duplicate checks, preservation, routing, and
wiki compilation. `second-brain-ingest` is a compatibility name for this same
workflow, not another pipeline. Read the internal
`references/wiki-compilation.md` when processing knowledge sources.

## Resolve the vault and schema first

Find the vault root from the current workspace and its nearest instruction files.
Read `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` relative to that root. It is the authority for
folder conventions, page metadata, provenance, index, log, and ingest/query/lint
workflows. Do not assume a saved Windows or Mac path. If no schema exists in the
target vault, report that gap before changing its structure; never initialize a
second vault to solve a request about an existing one.

## Route first

Identify what is being ingested, then jump to that section. If it is genuinely
ambiguous, ask once. Do not guess and file it somewhere wrong.

| Input | Where it lands | Section |
|---|---|---|
| Web clip from Obsidian Web Clipper (`.md` in `raw/inbox/`) | `raw/inbox/` | [Clips](#clips) |
| Public video or lecture clip | `raw/inbox/` | [Clips](#clips); acquire source captions if missing, never student intake |
| Student or consultation session transcript, live or archival | Student records | Delegate to `transcript-intake`; do not compile through this pipeline |
| Superwhisper `recordings/` dirs (`meta.json` + `output.wav`) or `settings.json` | wherever they were dropped | hand to `superwhisper`; not this skill's |
| Student evidence: PT score report, screenshot, homework export | `~/Downloads`, chat attachment | [Student evidence](#student-evidence) |
| A source document meant to become wiki knowledge | `raw/` | dedupe below, then read `references/wiki-compilation.md` |
| Takeout or platform dump | `raw/data-about-me/` | [Bulk exports](#bulk-exports) |

Student/session transcript intake is owned by `transcript-intake`.
Knowledge-source compilation is this skill's internal reference. `post-session-recap`
owns the complete student-facing session write-up workflow. A request only to
retrieve or file a transcript stays narrow; a recap request goes directly to that
skill without asking for a second activation.

   hash with completed wiki output means report the existing output and stop.
   An archived hash without finished output means resume compilation, not
   discard the source. Never delete duplicates.

33	Numbered Section Summaries  
34	  
35	1\. Why I Watch GitHub Now  
36	  
37	I treat GitHub as an early view of the agentic era. Many SaaS companies, agencies, workflows, and startup ideas start there first. I picked the repos from the ones that got attention over the last 30 days, and I explain each one in plain English.  
38	  
39	2\. No AI Slop Skill: Human Ideas First, AI Cleanup Second  
40	  
41	Peter Yang's skill strips the patterns that make writing smell like AI, and it keeps your actual voice. I care about this because founders write constantly: tweets, landing pages, cold emails, launch posts, onboarding copy, and investor updates. Readers who sense AI copy trust you less, so this repo makes your communication more believable.  
42	  
43	3\. The CRM Built For Agents  
44	  
45	The CRM by TryComp AI treats your CRM as a workspace for an agent instead of a filing cabinet for you. The agent researches people, enriches company data, schedules follow-ups, and keeps the notes current. Most businesses lose money because opportunities scatter across old emails, so a system that maintains the relationship graph for you is real revenue.  
46	  
47	4\. One Small Pipeline To Start With  
48	  
49	I would start with a single pipeline of warm leads that deserve a follow-up: everyone who replied, booked a call, asked about pricing, or promised to circle back. The install needs bun and docker, a git clone, the env example, and a few bun commands. It runs locally on localhost 3000 with the API on localhost 3001, and it uses Google or Microsoft OAuth for sign-in, email, and calendar.  
50	  
51	5\. Video Use: Turn Your Edit Style Into A System  
52	  
53	Video Use by browser use lets coding agents edit your raw footage. The agent removes filler words, cuts dead space, adds subtitles, color grades, builds overlays, renders the video, and then checks the output. Your edit style usually lives in muscle memory, and this repo makes it explicit and repeatable, which shrinks the distance between raw material and published content.  
54	  
55	6\. SkillSpector: Scan The Tool Before You Trust It

# Herdr gives many coding agents one organized, persistent home

Chase AI's video "This Repo Just Solved The #1 Claude + Codex Headache" ([YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=neK8ydl0Vlk), published 2026-07-16) presents Herdr, a free terminal multiplexer for running several coding agents at once. The full clip transcript was read on 2026-10-10 (about 10:18 of video). Herdr's repository is linked in the description as `github.com/ogulcancelik/herdr`; the captions mis-hear the name as "Herder" and "Hurder".

Evidence: [archived clip, provenance and hashes](../../raw/sources/clips/herdr-claude-codex-headache/PROVENANCE.md). Archived clip SHA-256 `44d232646d6de038bfeecc4e2496f01b60ce16b305b87b377fcfb7b8a8f47d2e`.

**This video was already ingested on 2026-09-22.** That ingest updated [Claude and Codex on one project](../../references/claude-plus-codex.md) and installed Herdr, but wrote no source summary and archived no clip. This page is the missing source summary, written from a newer caption generation. The provenance file lists all three clip copies and records an unresolved hash discrepancy for the 2026-09-22 file.

## What the video claims

All of this is the narrator's account, checked here only where noted.

- **Three benefits (0:31).** Organization (one window instead of many loose terminals), monitoring (a sidebar showing which agents are working, done or stuck), and persistence (Herdr runs as a server, so closing the window does not stop the agents; reopen and they are still running).
- **Versus tmux and Cmux (1:43 to 2:17).** The narrator says tmux does not show agent status out of the box, and that Cmux lacks persistence and is hard to use on Windows. He also says a running session can be reached from a phone or over SSH.
- **Install (2:33).** One line from `herdr.dev` or the GitHub page, then type `herdr`.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

# Turn AI clips into useful workspace knowledge

Joshua expects a substantial share of incoming clips to cover Claude, Codex, AI tips, prompts, repositories, new skills, product updates, and possible workspace improvements. This is the navigation and filing guide for that material. It is separate from the [LSAT teaching corpus](../../corpus/index.md), while remaining part of the same second brain.

This hub organizes existing authoritative pages rather than copying their knowledge into another set of pages. The intake rules remain in the [LLM Wiki schema](../../system/schema/llm-wiki.md), and the overall catalog remains [the wiki index](../../wiki/index.md).

[Recovered practical methods](migrated-source-methods.md) add the old vault's detailed artifact-review, skill-evaluation and evidence-checking recipes, plus its unimplemented annotation idea. [Migration coverage](../../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/active-migration-coverage.md) maps the original research and root files to current homes and explains exclusions.

[Karpathy source compilation](../../wiki/karpathy/index.md) now spans domain source pages and explicit semantic coverage. [Use the teaching setup](karpathy-method.md) for the reviewed rules, project agent, teaching skill and scoped Python execution gate. Compilation of the saved collection is complete according to the [current compilation audit](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). This does not establish exhaustive public-source acquisition or full understanding of binary, generated or media payloads. The [verified collection handoff](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/HANDOFF-VERIFICATION.md) records missing coverage, attribution limits and corrections to the original completeness claims.

[Resumable X collector](x-archive-collector.md) documents the free local script, signed-in persistent Chrome route, evidence ledgers, source-preserving repairs and retrieval limits.

13	
14	# Plan the visitor's decisions before the visual treatment
15	
16	Recovered method, adapted from the historical clip [I Built The Ultimate Claude Website Design Skill](<../raw/sources/old-obsidian-vault-2026-10-07/Clippings/I Built The Ultimate Claude Website Design Skill (steal this).md>). This is a planning reference, not a verified description of current software or an approved LSAT U redesign.
17	
18	1. State who arrives, what they currently believe, and what evidence would let them take the next step. A section needs a job in that sequence.
19	2. Outline the scroll journey before selecting components. Put explanation and proof where the visitor needs them, rather than filling a standard template.
20	3. Inventory available assets. Use real product screenshots and real evidence for factual claims. Treat generated imagery as illustration, with its purpose specified.
21	4. Vary visual intensity to support attention. Quiet explanation and a deliberate focal point can coexist; animation needs a reason tied to the visitor's task.
22	5. Choose any signature interaction around useful information or feedback. It should help the visitor understand or decide.
23	6. Check screenshots at meaningful positions and viewport sizes. Compare the rendered result with the intended sequence, readability, evidence, and next action.
24	
25	Use Joshua's existing product decisions and [teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md) for LSAT U work. This source supplies a design method, not authority to change curriculum, offers, branding, or publication state. Related intake and exclusions are recorded in the [research review](../research/obsidian-vault-comparison/2026-10-07/research-review.md).
26

## assistant

I ingested three of the four. The fourth isn't a clip: "7 Key UI Design Principles" is one paragraph of your own instructions for a homepage prompt ("Just give it complete creative freedom. Don't mention anything about logic games"), saved under a video title. I left it where it is, uncompiled.

Each of the three was read in full, then archived and hash-checked under `raw/sources/clips/`. The originals stay in `raw/`, untouched. Each now has a source summary in `wiki/sources/`.

**Nate Herk's two design videos, Fable 5.1 and GPT-6 Astra.** These fed into your existing [website planning page](references/website-design-planning.md) rather than becoming a parallel page. What they add:
- **Fit the visitor first.** His "pain, person, promise" check comes before any effect. A site is slop when it feels built for someone else, however polished it looks.
- **Give the model references, not adjectives.** "Make it premium" fails; name a reference site or borrow specific components.
- **Layer and give subtle scroll feedback** rather than piling on 3D.
- **Have the agent screenshot while scrolling** and fix what it sees. His own first drafts still had flaws.
- **Method matters more than the model** in his side-by-side tests.

**Greg Isenberg's five repos.** Summary only, since none of them is installed here. Two are worth acting on:
- **NVIDIA SkillSpector** scans skills for prompt injection and data theft before you trust them. More than 80 third-party skills went into this vault on 2026-10-12, so running its offline scan on those is my top suggestion.
- **No AI Slop** overlaps your writing rules and the `humanizer` skill. It's worth testing against `humanizer` on one real draft before adding it.

I installed and ran nothing for these suggestions.

- The originals' hashes are unchanged, and all three archives pass `sha256sum -c`. My first checksum files failed because of Windows line endings; I fixed them.
- The index is regenerated, and the log entry is added.
- The lint is back to the two dead links in old log entries that were already there.
